with
u\in\mathcal{I}
,
c\in\mathcal{O}
it is possible for the timer of the bound 500 to time out before the timer of the bound 100 does.
Repeated, this would lead to all timers for bound 100 being used, although only a single one is necessary.

Small note on Complexity.

In the worst case, for some formula
\varphi
we might have to generate timers for all possible indices, which are exponentially many in the size of the formula
|\varphi|
.
Hence,
|L|
might be triply exponential in
|\varphi|
, and
|\mathcal{V}|
can be doubly exponential in
|\varphi|
.
The state space
L\times\mathcal{V}
of the semantic safety game is triple exponential in
|\varphi|
, and hence also the worst-case solving time.
For formulas
\varphi
for which the generated timers only have maximum indices that are logarithmically bounded w.r.t. their duration,
|\mathcal{V}|
is only exponential in
|\varphi|
at worst.

Hybrid Expansion.

Generating all timers for a bound can have negative impact on the performance of solving the resulting game, due to large number of timers.
A remedy for this is to combine explicit expansion and timer-based implicit expansion.
To this end, we allow top-level temporal operators with numerical bounds, not just timers.
Then
\mathit{introExp}
has to be modified. For example

\mathit{iE^{\prime}}_{I}(\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 M 4.77 3.57 C 4.77 6.21 2.63 8.34 0 8.34 C -2.63 8.34 -4.77 6.21 -4.77 3.57 C -4.77 0.94 -2.63 -1.19 0 -1.19 C 2.63 -1.19 4.77 0.94 4.77 3.57 Z M 0 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[n]\varphi):=\begin{cases}\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 M 4.77 3.57 C 4.77 6.21 2.63 8.34 0 8.34 C -2.63 8.34 -4.77 6.21 -4.77 3.57 C -4.77 0.94 -2.63 -1.19 0 -1.19 C 2.63 -1.19 4.77 0.94 4.77 3.57 Z M 0 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[t_{I(n)}^{n}]\varphi&\lnot\mathit{Expandexplicit}\\
\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 M 4.77 3.57 C 4.77 6.21 2.63 8.34 0 8.34 C -2.63 8.34 -4.77 6.21 -4.77 3.57 C -4.77 0.94 -2.63 -1.19 0 -1.19 C 2.63 -1.19 4.77 0.94 4.77 3.57 Z M 0 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[n-1]\varphi&n>1,\mathit{Expandexplicit}\\
\mathit{iE^{\prime}}_{I}(\varphi)&n=1,\mathit{Expandexplicit}\end{cases}

Furthermore, this allows for handling directly operators like single
\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 M 4.77 3.57 C 4.77 6.21 2.63 8.34 0 8.34 C -2.63 8.34 -4.77 6.21 -4.77 3.57 C -4.77 0.94 -2.63 -1.19 0 -1.19 C 2.63 -1.19 4.77 0.94 4.77 3.57 Z M 0 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
.
In light of the complexity analysis above, we expand explicitly those bounds which generate more timers than their logarithm.
This does not change the complexity class, but reduces
|\mathcal{V}|
exponentially, with limited impact on the size of
L
, which improves the overall performance in practice.

Assumptions in
\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
.

Since
\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
does not include unbounded “eventually” operators, we cannot express
assumptions of the form
\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\psi
.
Due to this, we use an approximation to be able to handle simple assumptions of the form

\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\psi
where
\psi
is fully bounded, i.e., without
\LTLw
.
The idea is to expand the assumptions separately along with the other formulas.
If they become equivalent to
\bot
, we know for sure a violation has occurred and the system wins.
Otherwise they do not influence the outcome of the game.
This approximation is sound, i.e. if the system wins with approximation it would also win without, but not complete, i.e. if the system loses in the approximation we cannot make any statement.

Appendix 0.B
Proofs

0.B.1
Proofs from
Section

4

Theorem 0.B.1

Let
\Phi\in\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
and
G
be the countdown-timer game structure constructed from
\Phi
as described in
Section

4
.
Then there exists a system realizing
\mathcal{L}(\Phi)
if and only if the system wins in the countdown-timer game
(G,\mathit{UNSAFE}_{L})
.

Proof

The idea of the proof is to identify the semantic safety game of the given countdown game with expansions of
\Phi
as
\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
formulas.
Using these expansion we can then show that the safety game coincides with
\mathcal{L}(\Phi)
.

Let
G=(\mathcal{T},d,L,L_{0},\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O},\delta)
and let
(S,S_{0},\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O},\rho)
be the game structure of the semantic safety game of
G
according to
Definition

2
.

Note that
S
has the form
S=\mathit{PositiveBooleanCombinations}(\mathit{cl}(\Phi))\times\mathcal{V}
.
We associate each state in
S
with a formula in
\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
that is yielded by inserting the timer values as respective bounds.
Intuitively, we reverse the abstraction of the timers.
We define
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}^{t}\times\mathcal{V}\to\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
as

\displaystyle\langle l,v\rangle

\displaystyle:=l

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ l\in\{\mathit{ap},\lnot\mathit{ap}\}

\displaystyle\langle\varphi\penalty\ o\penalty\ \psi,v\rangle

\displaystyle:=\langle\varphi,v\rangle\penalty\ o\penalty\ \langle\psi,v\rangle

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ o\in\{\land,\lor\}

\displaystyle\langle\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[t]\varphi,v\rangle

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ v(t)>0

\displaystyle\langle\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle:=\bot

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ v(t)=0

\displaystyle\langle\varphi\LTLw[t]\psi,v\rangle

\displaystyle:=\langle\varphi,v\rangle\LTLw[v(t)-1]\langle\psi,v\rangle

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ v(t)>0

\displaystyle\langle\varphi\LTLw[t]\psi,v\rangle

\displaystyle:=\top

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ v(t)=0

\displaystyle\langle\varphi\LTLw\psi,v\rangle

\displaystyle:=\langle\varphi,v\rangle\LTLw\langle\psi,v\rangle

Furthermore, our argument is based on explicit expansion of
\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
formulas. Therefore, we define as single expansion and insertion set as step
\mathit{expand}:\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}\times(\mathcal{I}\cup\mathcal{O})\to\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
as

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\mathit{ap},m)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\top&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ \mathit{ap}\in m\\
\bot&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ \mathit{ap}\not\in m\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\lnot\mathit{ap},m)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\bot&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ \mathit{ap}\in m\\
\top&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ \mathit{ap}\not\in m\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\varphi\penalty\ o\penalty\ \psi,m)

\displaystyle:=\mathit{cfold}(\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m)\penalty\ o\penalty\ \mathit{expand}(\psi,m))

\displaystyle o\in\{\land,\lor\}

\displaystyle:=\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m)

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 M 4.77 3.57 C 4.77 6.21 2.63 8.34 0 8.34 C -2.63 8.34 -4.77 6.21 -4.77 3.57 C -4.77 0.94 -2.63 -1.19 0 -1.19 C 2.63 -1.19 4.77 0.94 4.77 3.57 Z M 0 3.57}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[n]\varphi,m)

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{\hbox to7.41pt{\vbox to7.41pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.70276pt\lower-1.11943pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ n>0

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\displaystyle:=\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m)

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }

\displaystyle:=\mathit{cfold}(\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m)\lor\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[n-1]\varphi)

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ n>0

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\varphi\LTLw[0]\psi,m)

\displaystyle:=\mathit{cfold}(\mathit{expand}(\psi,m)\lor\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m))

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\varphi\LTLw[n]\psi,m)

\displaystyle:=\mathit{cfold}(\mathit{expand}(\psi,m)\lor\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m)\land(\varphi\LTLw[n-1]\psi))

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ n>0

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}(\varphi\LTLw\psi,m)

\displaystyle:=\mathit{cfold}(\mathit{expand}(\psi,m)\lor\mathit{expand}(\varphi,m)\land(\varphi\LTLw\psi))

where constant folding is defined as

\displaystyle\mathit{cfold}(\top\land\psi)=\mathit{cfold}(\psi\land\top)=\psi

\displaystyle\mathit{cfold}(\bot\land\psi)=\mathit{cfold}(\psi\land\bot)=\bot

\displaystyle\mathit{cfold}(\bot\lor\psi)=\mathit{cfold}(\psi\lor\bot)=\psi

\displaystyle\mathit{cfold}(\top\lor\psi)=\mathit{cfold}(\psi\lor\top)=\top

Furthermore, for a trace
\tau\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{I}\cup\mathcal{O}}\right)}^{\omega}
, expansion up to step
k
is defined as inductively as

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}^{0}(\varphi,\tau)

\displaystyle:=\varphi

\displaystyle\mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi,\tau)

\displaystyle:=\mathit{expand}(\mathit{expand}^{k-1}(\varphi,\tau),\tau_{k-1})

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ k>0

For expansion, we can show the following (the proof follows later).

Lemma 1

For any trace
\tau\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{I}\cup\mathcal{O}}\right)}^{\omega}
, and formula
\varphi\in\mathit{SafeLTL}_{B}
:

\tau\not\vDash_{0}\varphi\Leftrightarrow\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi,\tau)=\bot

We now link expansion to the generated games.
We therefore assume for now that
\mathit{tree}
does no pruning, i.e. it only replaces Boolean top-level literals according to their values and does constant folding.
We argue later why the pruning is sound.

Let
\equiv
define equivalence of formulas on the Boolean top-level, then

Lemma 2

For any
v\in\mathcal{V}
, reachable
\varphi\in L
,
u\in 2^{\mathcal{I}}
, and

c\in 2^{\mathcal{O}}

\langle\rho((\varphi,v),u,c)\rangle\equiv\mathit{expand}(\langle\varphi,v\rangle,u\cup c)

The proof is given later.

We define the
k
-iteration of
\rho
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
on
s_{0}\in S
,
i\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{I}}\right)}^{\omega}
, and
o\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{O}}\right)}^{\omega}
as follows:

\displaystyle\rho^{0}(s_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=s_{0}

\displaystyle\rho^{k}(s_{0},\tau)

\displaystyle:=\rho(\rho^{k-1}(s_{0},\tau),i_{k-1},o_{k-1})

\displaystyle\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ k>0

For any
v\in\mathcal{V}
, reachable
\varphi\in L
,
i\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{I}}\right)}^{\omega}
, and
o\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{O}}\right)}^{\omega}
, iterating
Lemma

2
yields

\langle\rho^{k}((\varphi,v),i,o)\rangle\equiv\mathit{expand}^{k}(\langle\varphi,v\rangle,i\cup o)

for
k\in\mathbb{N}
where
i\cup o
is defined pointwise.
Note that this is only the case, as
\rho
and
\mathit{expand}
are semantic preserving, i.e. applying them to equivalent formulas provides an equivalent formula.
Combining this with
Lemma

1
yields

\displaystyle i\cup o\not\in\mathcal{L}(\langle\varphi,v\rangle)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow i\cup o\not\vDash_{0}\langle\varphi,v\rangle

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\langle\rho^{k}((\varphi,v),i,o)\rangle=\bot

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\rho^{k}((\varphi,v),i,o)\in\{\bot\}\times\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\rho^{k}((\varphi,v),i,o)\in\mathit{UNSAFE}

Note that
L_{0}=\{\mathit{opt}\mathit{introExp}(\Phi)\}
hence
S_{0}
is the singleton
S_{0}=\{s_{0}\}
with
s_{0}=(\mathit{opt}\mathit{introExp}(\Phi),\lambda t.\penalty\ d(t))
.
By construction of
\mathit{introExp}
and
\mathit{opt}
,
\Phi
is equivalent to
\langle s_{0}\rangle
, i.e.
\mathcal{L}(\Phi)=\mathcal{L}(\langle s_{0}\rangle s_{0})
, since
\mathit{introExp}
only applies expansion laws and insert timers that will be re-substituted by
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
accordingly.
Hence,

i\cup o\not\in\mathcal{L}(\Phi)\Leftrightarrow\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \rho^{k}(s_{0},i,o)\in\mathit{UNSAFE}

Since
i
and
o
are all-quantified, we just have to lift this statement in the context of systems and strategies.
We prove our main statement by showing that the environment wins in the game if and only if there is no implementation for
\mathcal{L}(\Phi)
.
If there is no implementation for
\mathcal{L}(\Phi)
then for all systems there exists an input sequence
i\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{I}}\right)}^{\omega}
such that for the resulting output sequence
o\in{\left(2^{\mathcal{O}}\right)}^{\omega}
, defined by
(c_{t+1},o_{t})=\gamma(c_{t},i_{t})
for
t\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\cup o\not\in\mathcal{L}(\Phi)
.
Using the above equivalence,
\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \rho^{k}(s_{0},i,o)\in\mathit{UNSAFE}
, i.e. any implementation looses the safety game.
Since safety games are solvable if and only if there are solvable with memory-less strategies the environment always wins the safety game.
Hence, the equivalence holds.

We now prove our two lemmas:

Proof
(
Lemma

1
)

Let
\tau[n]:=\lambda t.\penalty\ \tau_{t+n}
be
\tau
starting at position
n
.
We prove the more general statement

\forall t\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \tau\not\vDash_{t}\varphi\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi,\tau[t])=\bot

by structural induction over
\varphi
:

\mathit{ap}
:

\mathit{ap}\not\vDash_{t}\tau\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathit{ap}\not\in\tau_{t}\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathit{expand}(\mathit{ap},\tau[t])=\bot\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{1}(\mathit{ap},\tau[t])=\bot

\lnot\mathit{ap}
:

\lnot\mathit{ap}\not\vDash_{t}\tau\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathit{ap}\in\tau_{t}\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathit{expand}(\lnot\mathit{ap},\tau[t])=\bot\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{1}(\lnot\mathit{ap},\tau[t])=\bot

\varphi\land\psi
:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\tau\not\vDash_{t}\varphi\land\psi\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&(\tau\not\vDash_{t}\varphi)\lor(\tau\not\vDash_{t}\psi)\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&(\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi,\tau[t])=\bot)\\
&\lor(\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\psi,\tau[t])=\bot)&\penalty\ \text{by IH}\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi\land\psi,\tau[t])=\bot&\penalty\ \text{due to }\mathit{cfold}\end{array}

\varphi\lor\psi
:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\tau\not\vDash_{t}\varphi\lor\psi\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&(\tau\not\vDash_{t}\varphi)\land(\tau\not\vDash_{t}\psi)\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&(\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi,\tau[t])=\bot)\\
&\land(\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\psi,\tau[t])=\bot)&\penalty\ \text{by IH}\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi\lor\psi,\tau[t]\end{array}

Note that the last steps holds as when first disjunct becomes
\bot
constfold removes it.
The bound before the disjunction expands to
\bot
is the maximum out of both disjuncts.

\lxSVG@closescope }

The last step holds as
\mathit{expand}
will expand
\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

as disjuncts over all the
n
steps.
This is folded to
\bot
if an only if all sub-parts become
\bot
.

\varphi\LTLw\psi
:

\begin{array}[]{rll}&\tau\not\vDash_{t}\varphi\LTLw\psi\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&\exists j.(\tau\not\vDash_{t+j}\varphi)\land\forall j\leq j.(\tau\not\vDash_{t+i}\psi)\\
\penalty\ \Leftrightarrow&\exists j.(\exists k_{1}\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k_{1}}(\varphi,\tau[t+i]))\land\forall j\leq j.(\exists k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k_{2}}(\psi,\tau[t+i]))\end{array}

Note that

\mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi\LTLw\psi,\tau)=a_{0}\lor b_{0}\land(a_{1}\lor b_{1}\land(\dots(\varphi\LTLw\psi)))

where
a_{i}:=\mathit{expand}^{k-i}(\psi,\tau)
and
b_{i}=\mathit{expand}^{k-i}(\varphi,\tau)
are expansion of
\varphi
and
\psi
over the different expansions steps.
Now observe that this term can only be folded to
\bot
, if there is some expansion step
m
where
b_{m}
is
\bot
and all for all
i\leq m
,
a_{i}
is also
\bot
.
Hence, the above therm is equivalent to

\exists k\in\mathbb{N}.\penalty\ \mathit{expand}^{k}(\varphi\LTLw\psi,\tau[t])=\bot

Note that
k
is the maximum out of
k_{1}+i
and all
k_{2}+j
.

\varphi\LTLw[n]\psi
:

Analogous to the previous case but with a bound. If it is not satisfied then within the bound either
\varphi
is not satisfied and
\psi
was never satisfied.
When the respective terms in the expansion become
\bot
constant folding will fold everything to
\bot
.

∎

Proof
(
Lemma

2
)

\rho((\varphi,v),u,c)
is define as
(l^{\prime},v^{\prime})
where

•

T:=\{t\mid v(t)-1\leq 0\}

•

(e,\psi):=\mathit{squeeze}(\mathit{to}(T,\mathit{tree}(\varphi,u,c)))

•

l^{\prime}:=\mathit{opt}(\mathit{introExp}(\psi))
.

•

v^{\prime}:=\lambda t.\penalty\ \begin{cases}v(e(t))&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ e(t)\in\mathcal{T}\\
d(t)&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ e(t)=\mathit{RESET}\end{cases}

First note that
\mathit{squeeze}
only reorders timers such under a specific that
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
is invariant under the changes made in
v^{\prime}
.
As
\mathit{opt}
only modifies the top-level Boolean level, it remains to show that
\mathit{introExp}
,
\mathit{tree}
and
\mathit{to}
perform under
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
as
\mathit{expand}
.
First note that the time out behavior of
\mathit{to}
corresponds to the three cases with bounded operator cases with
n=0
in
\mathit{expand}
since in the timer game we use timers with incremented duration compared to the respective bound.

\mathit{to}
then just applies the neural-element which is remove by constant folding.

\mathit{introExp}
expands the formulas according to the expansion laws like
\mathit{expand}
for the other cases.
By construction, the introduction of timers is equivalent under
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
.

\mathit{tree}
assigns literals in the same cases
\mathit{expand}
does, namely only for top-level Boolean literals.
∎

It remains to show that the pruning made by
\mathit{tree}
is sound, i.e does not change how is winning.
For pruning rules (1) and (2) this is evident as in these cases the player can enforce enter their respective winning states.
For pruning rule (3) the system if forced to choose a successor
\psi_{G}
over
\psi_{B}
, such that
\mathcal{L}(\psi_{B})\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\psi_{G})
.
Hence, every strategy that looses for
\psi_{G}
looses for
\psi_{B}
, i.e. this does not change whether the environment wins.
Analogously, for pruning rule (4) is the system loose for the selected choice
\psi_{S}
of the rules the environment would just select this, if not then the system wins anyways as for the alternative choice of the environment
\psi_{N}
,
\mathcal{L}(\psi_{S})\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\psi_{N})
.∎

0.B.2
Proofs from
Section

5

Theorem 0.B.2

Solving countdown-timer games is
EXPTIME
-complete.

Proof

Inclusion in
EXPTIME
follows from the fact that building and solving the semantic safety game structure
G
can be done in time exponential in the size of the countdown-timer game structure
G_{T}
.
To see this, first note that the number of states
n_{s}=|L|\cdot|\mathcal{V}|
in the game
G
can be bounded by

n_{s}=|L|\cdot|\mathcal{V}|\leq|L|\cdot{(\max{d(t)\penalty\ :\penalty\ t\in\mathcal{T}})}^{|\mathcal{T}|}\leq|G_{T}|\cdot{(2^{|G_{T}|})}^{|G_{T}|}\leq 2^{\mathit{poly}(|G_{T}|)},

where
|G_{T}|
is the encoding length of the timer-game with numbers encoded in binary.
The number of transitions in
G
can be bounded by

n_{t}=n_{s}\cdot|2^{\mathcal{I}}|\cdot|2^{\mathcal{O}}|\leq 2^{\mathit{poly}(|G_{T}|)}.

Since the size of the game structure
G
is
n_{s}+n_{t}+|2^{\mathcal{I}}|+|2^{\mathcal{O}}|\leq 2^{\mathit{poly}(|G_{T}|)}
, and safety games can be solved in time linear in the size of the game graph, it follows that
solving
(G_{T},\mathit{UNSAFE})
can be done in exponential time.

We show
EXPTIME
-hardness by reduction from countdown games, introduced in
[
14
]
.
Countdown games are two-player games in which one player wins when they can make a transition after exactly
c
time units have elapsed, regardless of the behavior of the other player.
In
[
14
]
it was shown that the problem of determining the winning player in countdown games is
EXPTIME
-complete.

Formally, a countdown game is a pair

C=(S_{C},T_{C})
where

S_{C}
is a set of states and

T_{C}\subseteq S_{C}\times(\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0\})\times S_{C}
is the transition relation.
For
(s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\in T
,
\Delta
is called the duration of the transition.
A configuration of
C
is a pair
(s,c)
,
where
s\in S_{C}
and
c\in\mathbb{N}
.
The game proceeds as follows.
In a configuration
(s,c)
,
Player 1 chooses a number
\Delta
, such that

0<\Delta\leq c
and
(s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\in T_{C}
, for some state
s^{\prime}\in S_{C}
.
Then, Player 2 chooses a transition
(s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\in T_{C}

of duration
\Delta
.
The successor configuration is
(s^{\prime},c-\Delta)
.
There are two types of terminal configurations
(s,c)
in which no moves are available:

•

If
c=0
then
(s,c)
is terminal and is winning for Player 1.

•

If for all transitions
(s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\in T_{C}
we have that
\Delta>c
, then
(s,c)
is terminal and winning for Player 2.

The problem of solving countdown games is,
given a countdown game
C=(S_{C},T_{C})
and a configuration
(s,c)
,
with all the durations of transitions in
C
and the number
c
given in binary, to determine whether Player 1 has a strategy to enforce reaching a winning configuration from configuration
(s,c)
.
Deciding the winner in countdown games is
EXPTIME
-complete
[
14
]
.
We will show how to encode this problem into the problem of solving a countdown-timer game.

Let
C=(S_{C},T_{C})
be a countdown game and
(s,c)
be its initial configuration.
We assume w.l.o.g. that all durations are greater than to equal to
2
(this can be achieved by multiplying all durations and
c
by
2
).
We construct a timer game
(G_{T},\mathit{UNSAFE}_{L})
with

G_{T}=(\mathcal{T},d,L,L_{0},\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O},\delta)

such that the environment player wins
(G_{T},\mathit{UNSAFE}_{L})
if and only if Player 1 wins in the countdown game.
The game structure
G_{T}
is defined as follows:

•

\mathcal{T}:=\{g\}\cup\{t_{\Delta}\mid\exists s,s^{\prime}.\penalty\ (s,\Delta+1,s^{\prime})\in T_{C}\}
with duration
d(g):=c
and
d(t_{\Delta})=\Delta
.
The timer
g
is used to model the global countdown value in a configuration.
The other timers are used to model the durations of the transitions.
Note that their duration is reduced by one, which is because we have to introduce auxiliary transitions.

•

L:=S_{C}\cup T_{C}\cup\{\mathit{SAFE},\mathit{UNSAFE}\}
.

–

The locations
S_{C}
are the respective states in the countdown game.

–

The locations
T_{C}
are auxiliary states.
In these auxiliary states, the value of
g
is reduced by the respective duration of the transition in the countdown game, by staying as long as necessary in the corresponding state (using some of the timers
t_{\Delta}
).

–

Locations
\mathit{SAFE}
and
\mathit{UNSAFE}
represent the terminal states of the game (and are used to avoid spurious choices in the transition relation).

•

We let
\mathcal{I}:=\mathit{Propositions}(C)
, where
\mathit{Propositions}(C)
are propositions corresponding to the binary encoding of durations in
C
. Thus,

2^{\mathcal{I}}\supseteq\{\Delta\mid\exists s,s^{\prime}.\penalty\ (s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\in T_{C}\},

and the elements of
2^{\mathcal{I}}
represent the actions of Player 1 in
C
.
The spurious choices are handled by
\delta
by redirecting to
\mathit{SAFE}
.

•

We let
\mathcal{O}:=\mathit{Propositions}(T_{C})
, where
\mathit{Propositions}(T_{C})
are propositions corresponding to the binary encoding of the transitions
T_{C}
.
Thus,

2^{\mathcal{O}}\supseteq T_{C},

and the elements of
2^{\mathcal{O}}
represent the actions of Player 2 in
C
.
The spurious choices are handled by
\delta
by redirecting to
\mathit{UNSAFE}
.

In
G_{T}
we use the following effects:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathit{ea}&:=\lambda t.\penalty\ \mathit{RESET}\\
\mathit{eg}&:=\lambda t.\penalty\ \begin{cases}g&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ t=g\\
\mathit{RESET}&\penalty\ \text{otherwise}\end{cases}\\
e_{\Delta}&:=\lambda t.\penalty\ \begin{cases}g&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ t\in\{g,t_{\Delta}\}\\
\mathit{RESET}&\penalty\ \text{otherwise}\end{cases}\end{array}

The transition relation
\delta
is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\delta(s,i,o,\mathit{TO})&:=(s,\mathit{ea})&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ s\in\{\mathit{SAFE},\mathit{UNSAFE}\}\\
\delta(s,\Delta,t_{C},\mathit{TO})&:=\begin{cases}(\mathit{SAFE},\mathit{ea})&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ \lnot\exists s^{\prime}\in S.\penalty\ (s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\in T_{C}\\
(\mathit{UNSAFE},\mathit{ea})&\penalty\ \text{otherwise if}\penalty\ \lnot\exists s^{\prime}\in S_{C}.\penalty\ t_{C}=(s,\Delta,s^{\prime})\\
(t_{C},\mathit{eg})&\penalty\ \text{otherwise if}\penalty\ g\not\in\mathit{TO}\\
(\mathit{SAFE},\mathit{ea})&\penalty\ \text{otherwise if}\penalty\ g\in\mathit{TO}\\
\end{cases}&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ s\in S_{C}\\
\delta(t_{C},i,o,\mathit{TO})&:=\mathit{let}\penalty\ (s,\Delta,s^{\prime}):=t_{C}\penalty\ \mathit{in}\\
&\begin{cases}(t_{C},e_{\Delta})&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ g,t_{\Delta-1}\not\in\mathit{TO}\\
(s^{\prime},\mathit{eg})&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ g\not\in\mathit{TO}\land t_{\Delta-1}\in\mathit{TO}\\
(\mathit{SAFE},\mathit{ea})&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ g\in\mathit{TO}\land t_{\Delta-1}\not\in\mathit{TO}\\
(\mathit{UNSAFE},\mathit{ea})&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ g,t_{\Delta-1}\in\mathit{TO}\end{cases}&\penalty\ \text{if}\penalty\ t_{C}\in T_{C}\end{array}

The transition relation models the moves of Player 1 and Player 2 in
C
as the choices of the environment player and the system player in
G_{T}
respectively.

The reduction given above is polynomial.
By construction, the environment player wins
(G_{T},\mathit{UNSAFE})
if and only if Player 1 wins
C
.
Thus, we can conclude that solving countdown-timer games is
EXPTIME
-hard.

This completes the proof of
EXPTIME
-completness.

Figure 5
:
Countdown-timer game with
L=\{\mathit{start},\mathit{warm}\text{-}\mathit{up},\mathit{print}\}
.
For convenience, we split each transition in two parts.
The first is labeled with a Boolean formula
\alpha
over
\mathcal{I}\cup\mathcal{O}
.
The second, with a pair
T:e
, where
T\subseteq 2^{\mathcal{T}}
is a set of sets of timers that time out in the given step, and
e\in\mathcal{E}
is an effect.
A pair of labels
\alpha
,
T:e
connecting locations
l
and
l^{\prime}
represents the fact that there are
(i,o)\in 2^{\mathcal{I}}\times 2^{\mathcal{I}}
that satisfy
\alpha
and
\tau\in T
, such that
\delta(l,i,o,\tau)=(l^{\prime},e)
.
We denote
\text{reset-all}:=2^{\mathcal{T}}:e_{\mathit{reset}}
where
e_{\mathit{reset}}(t)=\mathit{RESET}
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}
.
For simplicity, we omit the irrelevant label elements of the transitions to the unsafe state
\bot
.

Appendix 0.D
Benchmarks

0.D.1
Office Robot Benchmarks

Cleaning robot for
N\in\{1,2,3,4\}
offices

•

\mathcal{I}=\emptyset

•

\mathcal{O}=\{\mathit{corridor},\mathit{office}_{1},\mathit{office}_{2},\mathit{office}_{3},\mathit{office}_{4}\}

•

\Phi_{\mathit{Clean}(N)}:=\varphi_{\mathit{start}}\wedge\varphi_{\mathit{position}}\wedge\varphi_{\mathit{mutex}}\wedge\varphi_{\mathit{clean-duration}}\wedge\varphi_{\mathit{clean}}^{N}

–

The robot starts in the corridor.

\varphi_{\mathit{start}}:=\mathit{corridor}

–

The robot is in at least one location.

\varphi_{\mathit{position}}:=\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

[

]

The exact benchmarks were taken from
https://gitlab.fbk.eu/lgeatti/ksy-tool.git
.
They consist of four formula types
\varphi_{A}(N),\varphi_{B}(N),\varphi_{C}(N),\varphi_{D}(N)
with
N\in\{2,\dots,200\}
.
\varphi_{A}
,
\varphi_{B}
are realizable and
\varphi_{C}
,
\varphi_{D}
are unrealizable.

\mathcal{I}:=\{u_{0},\ldots,u_{N}\}
and
\mathcal{O}:=\{c0,\ldots,cN\}
.
